 def validate_round_cost_smoke() -> None:
     print("[7/8] Round cost settlement smoke test (round_cost.py)")
     cost_script = LOOP_SCRIPTS / "round_cost.py"
     smoke_root = REPO_ROOT / ".tmp" / f"cost-smoke-{uuid.uuid4().hex}"
     project = smoke_root / "project"
     transcripts = smoke_root / "transcripts"
     try:
         project.mkdir(parents=True)
         transcripts.mkdir(parents=True)
         turn = {
             "type": "assistant",
             "message": {
                 "usage": {
                     "input_tokens": 100,
                     "cache_creation_input_tokens": 50,
                     "cache_read_input_tokens": 1000,
                     "output_tokens": 200,
                 },
                 "content": [{"type": "text", "text": "updating .harnessloop/state/current.md"}],
             },
         }
         business_turn = json.loads(json.dumps(turn))
         business_turn["message"]["content"] = [{"type": "text", "text": "business work"}]
 
         # Same-id multi-line usage: Claude Code writes one JSONL line per
         # content block of a single assistant message, and every line for
         # that message.id repeats the message's usage. round_cost.py must
         # dedupe by message.id instead of summing every line, or these two
         # lines for msg_dup001 (still "open" - no closing message after them
         # yet, mirroring round_cost.py normally being invoked from inside
         # the very message it is reporting on) would be billed twice each
         # settlement they're re-scanned in. These assertions fail against
         # the pre-fix per-line-summing logic.
         dup_block_1 = {
             "type": "assistant",
             "message": {
                 "id": "msg_dup001",
                 "usage": {
                     "input_tokens": 300,
                     "cache_creation_input_tokens": 20,
                     "cache_read_input_tokens": 400,
                     "output_tokens": 50,
                 },
                 "content": [{"type": "thinking", "thinking": "planning the change"}],
             },
         }
         dup_block_2 = json.loads(json.dumps(dup_block_1))
         dup_block_2["message"]["usage"]["output_tokens"] = 120
         dup_block_2["message"]["content"] = [{"type": "tool_use", "name": "Bash", "input": {}}]
 
         (transcripts / "session.jsonl").write_text(
             json.dumps(turn) + "\n"
             + "garbage-line\n"
             + json.dumps(business_turn) + "\n"
             + json.dumps(dup_block_1) + "\n"
             + json.dumps(dup_block_2) + "\n",
             encoding="utf-8",
         )
 
         result = run_python(cost_script, "--project", str(project), "--transcript-dir", str(transcripts))
         check(result.returncode == 0, "round_cost.py exits 0 on synthetic transcript")
         check("## Cost" in result.stdout, "round_cost.py emits a ## Cost section")
         check("2 assistant turn(s)" in result.stdout, "round_cost.py counts assistant turns")
         check("1/2 turns" in result.stdout, "round_cost.py attributes protocol turns heuristically")
         check(
             "Output tokens: 400" in result.stdout,
             "round_cost.py defers the still-open msg_dup001 message instead of billing its partial lines",
         )
 
         marker_path = project / ".harnessloop" / "local" / "cost-marker.json"
         check(marker_path.exists(), "round_cost.py writes the settlement marker")
         marker = json.loads(marker_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
         pending = marker.get("files", {}).get("session.jsonl", {})
         check(
             pending.get("offset") == 5
             and pending.get("pending_id") == "msg_dup001"
             and pending.get("pending_usage") == {"input": 300, "cache_write": 20, "cache_read": 400, "output": 120},
             "marker carries the still-open message.id and its max-merged usage across the settlement window",
         )
 
         # Continue the session: msg_dup001's 3rd (final) content-block line
         # arrives, then a genuinely new message (msg_next002) closes it out.
         # This is the cross-settlement-window case: msg_dup001's lines are
         # split across two round_cost.py runs by the marker offset above,
         # and must still be billed exactly once (using its max usage, not
         # the sum of all 3 lines: 50 + 120 + 150 = 320 would be wrong).
         dup_block_3 = json.loads(json.dumps(dup_block_1))
         dup_block_3["message"]["usage"]["output_tokens"] = 150
         dup_block_3["message"]["content"] = [
             {"type": "text", "text": "done, see .harnessloop/round-summary.md"}
         ]
         next_turn = {
             "type": "assistant",
             "message": {
                 "id": "msg_next002",
                 "usage": {
                     "input_tokens": 77,
                     "cache_creation_input_tokens": 5,
                     "cache_read_input_tokens": 10,
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Verify the mechanical subset of the Harnessloop protocol.
     3	
     4	Judgment gates (does this evidence support acceptance?) stay with the model.
     5	This script enforces only machine-checkable rules:
     6	
     7	- Rule A (scope-lock containment): every file under a round's evidence/ and
     8	  reviews/ directories must fall inside a path allowed by that round's
     9	  scope-lock.md "Allowed Changes" section.
    10	- Rule B (citation existence): every backtick-quoted span in a round's
    11	  review files that looks like a project-relative path must exist in the
    12	  project. A span is exempt from this check (not treated as a citation) if
    13	  any of the following hold:
    14	    - it contains a regex/glob metacharacter (^ $ * ? | ( ) [ ] { } \\ +),
    15	      e.g. a quoted pattern like `^_?\\s*(no|none)\\b` or a shell glob like
    16	      `__pycache__/*.pyc` — these are not literal paths;
    17	    - it opens with a bare (schemeless) domain followed by a path, e.g.
    18	      `docs.python.org/3/library/os.html` or `github.com/org/repo` — full
    19	      `scheme://` URLs were already exempt, this extends that to the bare
    20	      form reviewers commonly write when citing external docs;
    21	    - it contains an angle-bracket placeholder anywhere, e.g.
    22	      `goals/<id>/data-contract.md` — this describes the *shape* of a path
    23	      template, not a literal reference;
    24	    - the citation's line, or the line immediately before it, carries an
    25	      explicit `<!-- verify:ignore -->` HTML comment — use this to mark a
    26	      review line whose citation is known-intentional prose (e.g. quoting
    27	      another document's typo verbatim) rather than a real reference. This
    28	      is the escape hatch for cases the heuristics above cannot resolve
    29	      mechanically; it does not require a project-level opt-out.
    30	
    31	  Existence is checked against the project root, the round's goal
    32	  directory, the round directory itself, the project's own `.harnessloop/`
    33	  directory (so a citation using one of the PATHISH_PREFIXES verbatim,
    34	  e.g. `setup/data-sources.md` or `state/self-check.md`, resolves against
    35	  where those directories actually live), and the root of every
    36	  git submodule declared in the project's .gitmodules — including nested
    37	  ones (e.g. `path = kernels/openclaw`), so a citation written relative to
    38	  a submodule's own root, e.g. `plugins/foo/` when `foo` is checked out as
    39	  a submodule at <project>/foo or <project>/kernels/foo, resolves
    40	  correctly instead of being flagged as dangling).
    41	
    42	  Before existence is checked, a trailing locator suffix is stripped from
    43	  the citation: `:<line>`, `:<start>-<end>`, or `::<anchor>` (see
    44	  `strip_locator_suffix`). Reviewers routinely cite `path/to/file.py:123`
    45	  or `doc.md::some-anchor` — the locator addresses a position *within* an
    46	  already-real file, it is not part of the file's path, and checking the
    47	  literal locator-suffixed string against the filesystem always fails.
    48	  Both the original and the stripped form are tried, in that order, so a
    49	  citation that happens to end in something that is coincidentally not a
    50	  locator is not silently mis-resolved.
    51	
    52	  Every base resolution (project root, goal/round dirs, `.harnessloop/`,
    53	  submodule roots, and the suffix fallback below) is *containment-checked*
    54	  using canonical (symlink-resolved) paths on both sides, not merely a
    55	  lexical `normpath` comparison: the candidate and the project root are
    56	  each passed through `Path.resolve(strict=False)` before the containment
    57	  test, and the candidate must land inside the resolved project root (see
    58	  `_is_contained`). A citation containing `../` that would otherwise walk
    59	  outside the project (e.g. `../outside/ghost.py`), a `.gitmodules`
    60	  `path =` entry that points outside the project root, or a
    61	  project-internal symlink (a submodule-root path itself, an explicit-base
    62	  candidate, or a suffix-index hit) whose real target lives outside the
    63	  project tree, is never treated as resolved — even if something of that
    64	  name happens to exist on the host filesystem just outside the project
    65	  tree (TH-0008 REWORK: `submodule_parent_escape`; T-063 MUST-FIX 2:
    66	  `symlink_containment_escape` — lexical `normpath` containment alone
    67	  passes a symlink whose path is inside the project but whose target is
    68	  not, and that held across all three of the base-resolution, submodule-root,
    69	  and suffix-match paths, not just one). Resolving the project root too
    70	  (not only the candidate) matters even absent any project-authored
    71	  symlink: on macOS, e.g., `/tmp` is itself a symlink to `/private/tmp`, so
    72	  a project rooted under `/tmp` would otherwise be compared against an
    73	  unresolved parent and see spurious mismatches. A broken symlink's
    74	  `resolve(strict=False)` does not raise — it normalizes past the point the
    75	  target stops existing — so containment alone does not double as an
    76	  existence check; the existing match-time re-verification (`_exists_as`,
    77	  `broken_symlink` / `stale_index_after_delete`) still owns that job and is
    78	  always applied in addition to, not instead of, containment.
    79	
    80	  If a citation still does not resolve against any of the bases above
    81	  (after locator-stripping), it is tried once more as a path *suffix*
    82	  against the project's indexed files (see `build_suffix_index` /
    83	  `suffix_unique_match`): this is the fallback for a citation that is
    84	  correct but written relative to neither the project root, the
    85	  round/goal directory, `.harnessloop/`, nor any submodule root — e.g.
    86	  `harnessloop-setup/SKILL.md` for a file that actually lives at
    87	  `plugins/harnessloop/skills/harnessloop-setup/SKILL.md`. This fallback is
    88	  deliberately conservative: it requires at least two path segments (a
    89	  single bare filename never qualifies — that shape is both the most
    90	  common typo and the least informative citation), compares whole path
    91	  *segments* rather than raw string suffixes (so `os.html` cannot match
    92	  `macos.html`), and only exempts the citation when exactly one indexed
    93	  file matches that suffix *and* the specific path that match names still
    94	  actually exists (or, for a citation ending in `/`, is still actually a
    95	  directory) at the moment of checking — zero or multiple matches both
    96	  still report `dangling-citation`, and so does a unique match whose
    97	  target has since disappeared or was never real to begin with (a broken
    98	  symlink that `os.walk` lists in `filenames` without following it, or an
    99	  index entry for a file deleted after the index was built but before this
   100	  citation was checked; TH-0008 REWORK: `broken_symlink`,
   101	  `stale_index_after_delete`).
   102	
   103	  The index backing this fallback is built from every file that genuinely
   104	  exists in the project's worktree and is not gitignored — tracked *and*
   105	  untracked-but-not-ignored (`git ls-files -z --cached --recurse-submodules`
   106	  plus `git ls-files -z --others --exclude-standard`, the latter extended
   107	  into nested submodules via `git submodule foreach --recursive` since
   108	  `--others` itself has no `--recurse-submodules` mode — run from the
   109	  project root, when the project root is itself a git working-tree top
   110	  level) — falling back to walking the filesystem (pruning
   111	  `NOISE_DIR_NAMES`) only when the project is not a git working-tree root or
   112	  `git` is unavailable. "Unique" means unique among this real-worktree,
   113	  non-ignored surface, not literally "git-tracked": an earlier rework
   114	  narrowed the index to tracked files only, which closed the noise-pruning
   115	  blind spot below but opened a new one — a round's own freshly-produced
   116	  evidence/review files are untracked by construction, so a genuine
   117	  same-suffix collision between a tracked file and such a fresh untracked
   118	  one was invisible to the ambiguity check, making a citation that should
   119	  have been multiply-resolvable look uniquely resolved instead (T-063
   120	  MUST-FIX 1: `untracked_pseudo_unique`). A gitignored file colliding with a
   121	  tracked/untracked file's suffix is still invisible to the index either
   122	  way (that generated-output exclusion is the point of `--exclude-standard`),
   123	  and — unlike the prior walk-based index — a real, non-ignored source
   124	  directory that happens to share a name with an entry in `NOISE_DIR_NAMES`
   125	  (e.g. a project that genuinely has a source directory named `build/`) is
   126	  indexed and can correctly participate in an ambiguity instead of being
   127	  silently pruned out of consideration (TH-0008 REWORK:
   128	  `noise_pruned_ambiguity`). The walk-based fallback, when it is used, still
   129	  prunes `NOISE_DIR_NAMES` and is subject to the same pruning blind spot it
   130	  always was.
   131	
   132	  A citation ending in `/` names a directory, not a file: it only resolves
   133	  when the matched filesystem entry is actually a directory (`is_dir()`),
   134	  never when it merely shares a path with an existing file of the same
   135	  name (TH-0008 REWORK: `trailing_slash_file` — `pkg/real.md/` must not
   136	  resolve against the file `pkg/real.md`). This directory semantics is
   137	  applied uniformly to explicit-base resolution and to the suffix
   138	  fallback.
   139	
   140	  A citation that starts with `~/` (home-relative), `/` (POSIX filesystem
   141	  absolute), or a Windows-style absolute path — a drive letter
   142	  (`C:/...`, written `C:\\...` before backslashes are normalized to `/`)
   143	  or a UNC path (`\\\\server\\share\\...`, normalized to `//server/share/...`,
   144	  which already starts with `/` and so falls under the same check) — is
   145	  exempt from existence checking entirely, for the same reason a
   146	  `scheme://` URL is exempt: it names a location outside the project tree,
   147	  and Rule B has no project-declared base to resolve it against —
   148	  inventing one (e.g. treating `/` as the OS root, or `C:/` as a Windows
   149	  drive root) would let the check silently walk outside the project, or
   150	  behave differently depending on which OS the check happens to run on.
   151	  This is a real, known gap: a review that cites a path inside an external
   152	  design wiki kept outside this project (e.g. under a user's
   153	  `~/.llm-wiki/...`) is never verified, because harnessloop has no way to
   154	  know where — or whether — that external tree exists. Closing that gap
   155	  requires a project to declare an additional resolution base for such
   156	  external stores; that is a separate, project-level protocol decision and
   157	  is out of scope here. Unlike before, this exemption is no longer silent:
   158	  every span skipped for this reason is counted in the `coverage` dict's
   159	  `citations_exempt_external` field (see `_empty_coverage`), so a run's
   160	  coverage line makes visible how much of its citation surface was waved
   161	  through unchecked rather than hiding that number entirely.
   162	
   163	  Two honesty notes on what this rule cannot mechanically guarantee even
   164	  after the above:
   165	
   166	  - Case sensitivity of the final existence check is whatever the host
   167	    filesystem gives it, not a Rule B decision: `Path.exists()` /
   168	    `is_dir()` are case-sensitive on a typical Linux filesystem and
   169	    case-insensitive (but case-preserving) by default on macOS and
   170	    Windows. A citation with the wrong case for a real file can therefore
   171	    pass or fail depending on which machine runs the check — this rule
   172	    does not normalize case itself and cannot make that determinism
   173	    project-wide.
   174	  - The suffix-unique fallback proves "exactly one indexed file has this
   175	    suffix", not "this is the file the reviewer meant to cite". A
   176	    reviewer who mistypes `mistyped/config.yaml` when they meant a
   177	    different file will still get a pass if `vendor/mistyped/config.yaml`
   178	    happens to be the tree's only file ending in `mistyped/config.yaml` —
   179	    the algorithm cannot distinguish a lucky coincidental match from the
   180	    intended one. This is inherent to any suffix-based fallback and is not
   181	    something this rework (or any purely mechanical suffix match) can
   182	    close; it is recorded here rather than fixed because fixing it would
   183	    require either dropping the fallback (reintroducing the false
   184	    positives it exists to remove) or requiring citations to be written
   185	    unambiguously (a review-authoring discipline change, not a Rule B one).
   186	
   187	Exit codes: 0 = pass, 1 = violations found, 2 = usage error.
   188	"""
   189	
   190	from __future__ import annotations
   191	
   192	import argparse
   193	import json
   194	import os
   195	import re
   196	import subprocess
   197	import sys
   198	from pathlib import Path
   199	
   200	CODE_SPAN = re.compile(r"`([^`]+)`")
   201	PATHISH_PREFIXES = (
   202	    ".harnessloop/",
   203	    "rounds/",
   204	    "evidence/",
   205	    "reviews/",
   206	    "goals/",
   207	    "state/",
   208	    "setup/",
   209	    "meta/",
   210	    "evals/",
   211	    "intake/",
   212	)
   213	
   214	# Explicit per-line opt-out for Rule B: a review line that carries this
   215	# marker (or is immediately preceded by a line that does) has every
   216	# citation on it skipped. See module docstring for when to use it.
   217	IGNORE_MARKER = "<!-- verify:ignore -->"
   218	
   219	# Regex/glob metacharacters. A backtick span containing any of these is a
   220	# quoted pattern (regex alternation, anchors, glob wildcards, ...), not a
   220	# quoted pattern (regex alternation, anchors, glob wildcards, ...), not a
   221	# literal path, so it is never treated as a citation.
   222	PATH_META_CHARS = frozenset("^$*?|(){}[]\\+")
   223	
   224	# A bare (schemeless) domain: at least two dot-separated alnum/hyphen
   225	# labels, e.g. "docs.python.org" or "github.com". Anchored so it must
   226	# consume the whole first path segment — this deliberately does not match
   227	# a plain filename with an extension (e.g. "package.json" has no `/`
   228	# after it, and is never even offered to this check; see
   229	# _looks_like_bare_domain).
   230	BARE_DOMAIN_RE = re.compile(
   231	    r"^[A-Za-z0-9](?:[A-Za-z0-9-]*[A-Za-z0-9])?"
   232	    r"(?:\.[A-Za-z0-9](?:[A-Za-z0-9-]*[A-Za-z0-9])?)+$"
   233	)
   234	
   235	# A Windows drive-letter absolute path, e.g. `C:/Users/x` -- checked after
   236	# `pathish_citations` has already normalized backslashes to `/`, so the
   237	# original `C:\Users\x` form is matched by this too. See
   238	# `_looks_like_out_of_project`.
   239	WINDOWS_DRIVE_ABS_RE = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z]:/")
   240	
   241	# Trailing locator suffixes stripped before existence checking: `::anchor`
   242	# (checked first, since it is the more specific shape) and `:line` /
   243	# `:start-end`. See `strip_locator_suffix`.
   244	ANCHOR_SUFFIX_RE = re.compile(r"::[^:]+$")
   245	LINE_SUFFIX_RE = re.compile(r":\d+(?:-\d+)?$")
   246	
   247	# Noise directories excluded from the Rule B suffix-unique fallback index
   248	# (`build_suffix_index`): build output, vendored/installed dependencies, and
   249	# VCS internals are never what a review is citing, and including them would
   250	# both slow the index and manufacture spurious "unique" matches inside
   251	# generated trees that happen to mirror source filenames.
   252	NOISE_DIR_NAMES = frozenset(
   253	    {
   254	        ".git",
   255	        "node_modules",
   256	        "dist",
   257	        "build",
   258	        "bin",
   259	        "obj",
   260	        ".venv",
   261	        "__pycache__",
   262	        ".artifacts",
   263	        "coverage",
   264	    }
   265	)
   266	
   267	
   268	def norm(path: Path) -> str:
   269	    return os.path.normcase(os.path.normpath(str(path)))
   270	
   271	
   272	def is_under(child: Path, parent: Path) -> bool:
   273	    child_s, parent_s = norm(child), norm(parent)
   274	    return child_s == parent_s or child_s.startswith(parent_s + os.sep)
   275	
   276	
   277	def _canonical(path: Path) -> Path:
   278	    """Resolve `path` to its canonical (symlink-following) form.
   279	
   280	    `strict=False` normalizes past a broken symlink or a nonexistent
   281	    component instead of raising — existence is a separate concern, owned
   282	    by `_exists_as` at the point a match is actually accepted, not by this
   283	    resolution step (T-063 MUST-FIX 2: canonical containment and match-time
   284	    existence re-verification must not be conflated with each other).
   285	    """
   286	    return path.resolve(strict=False)
   287	
   288	
   289	def _is_contained(candidate: Path, project: Path) -> bool:
   290	    """True if `candidate` lands inside `project` under *canonical*
   291	    (symlink-resolved) comparison of both sides.
   292	
   293	    Lexical `normpath` containment (`is_under` alone) is not enough: a
   294	    project-internal symlink whose path is inside the project but whose
   295	    target is not (e.g. `<project>/link -> /outside`, or a `.gitmodules`
   296	    `path =` entry naming such a symlink) passes a lexical check while
   297	    actually resolving outside the project entirely (T-063 MUST-FIX 2:
   298	    `symlink_containment_escape`). `project` itself is resolved too, not
   299	    just `candidate` — on macOS `/tmp` is a symlink to `/private/tmp`, so a
   300	    project rooted there would otherwise be compared against an unresolved
   301	    parent and see a spurious mismatch (or, worse, a spurious escape) for
   302	    every candidate. Used by every containment check that guards Rule B
   303	    citation resolution: `_resolve_in_project` (explicit-base and suffix
   304	    resolution), `submodule_roots` (`.gitmodules` `path =` acceptance), and
   305	    `suffix_unique_match` (the unique-hit's specific path) — all three must
   306	    share this one definition so a symlink escape cannot slip through
   307	    whichever path is not updated.
   308	    """
   309	    return is_under(_canonical(candidate), _canonical(project))
   310	
   311	
   312	def extract_allowed_spans(scope_lock_text: str) -> list[str]:
   313	    """Collect allowed paths from the '## Allowed Changes' section.
   314	
   315	    Accepts both formats in the wild: backtick path spans in prose/bullets,
   316	    and the scope-lock-template.md table whose first column is the path.
   317	    """
   318	    spans: list[str] = []
   319	    in_section = False
   320	    for line in scope_lock_text.splitlines():
   321	        stripped = line.strip()
   322	        if stripped.startswith("## "):
   323	            in_section = stripped.lower() == "## allowed changes"
   324	            continue
   325	        if not in_section:
   326	            continue
   327	        spans.extend(CODE_SPAN.findall(line))
   328	        if stripped.startswith("|"):
   329	            cells = [c.strip() for c in stripped.strip("|").split("|")]
   330	            if not cells:
   331	                continue
   332	            first = cells[0].strip("`").strip()
   333	            if (
   334	                first
   335	                and not set(first) <= {"-", ":", " "}
   336	                and first.lower() not in {"path/data/tool", "path", "file", "target"}
   337	                and " " not in first
   338	            ):
   339	                spans.append(first)
   340	    return sorted({s.strip().replace("\\", "/") for s in spans if s.strip()})
   341	
   342	
   343	def _looks_like_pattern(cleaned: str) -> bool:
   344	    """True if the span contains a regex/glob metacharacter.
   345	
   346	    A quoted regex (e.g. ``^_?\\s*(no|none)\\b.*declared.*_?$``) or a shell
   347	    glob (e.g. ``__pycache__/*.pyc``) is not a literal path reference, even
   348	    though it may contain slashes and look pathish otherwise.
   349	    """
   350	    return any(ch in PATH_META_CHARS for ch in cleaned)
   351	
   352	
   353	def _looks_like_bare_domain(cleaned: str) -> bool:
   354	    """True if the span opens with a schemeless domain followed by a path.
   355	
   356	    Matches things like ``docs.python.org/3/library/os.html`` or
   357	    ``github.com/org/repo/blob/main/src/foo.py``. Requires a `/` after the
   358	    domain-looking head so plain filenames with an extension (e.g.
   359	    ``package.json``, which has no trailing path segment) are never
   360	    considered here.
   361	    """
   362	    if "/" not in cleaned:
   363	        return False
   364	    head = cleaned.split("/", 1)[0]
   365	    return bool(BARE_DOMAIN_RE.match(head))
   366	
   367	
   368	def _looks_like_placeholder(cleaned: str) -> bool:
   369	    """True if the span contains an angle-bracket placeholder anywhere.
   370	
   371	    Templated paths such as ``goals/<id>/data-contract.md`` describe a
   372	    *shape* of path, not a literal reference — the leading-character check
   373	    below only catches a span that *starts* with `<`; a placeholder in the
   374	    middle of an otherwise pathish span (e.g. after a real prefix like
   375	    `goals/`) needs its own check.
   376	    """
   377	    return "<" in cleaned or ">" in cleaned
   378	
   379	
   380	def _looks_like_out_of_project(cleaned: str) -> bool:
   381	    """True if the span is home-relative (``~/...``), POSIX filesystem-
   382	    absolute (``/...``, which also covers a UNC path once
   383	    `pathish_citations` has normalized its backslashes to slashes, e.g.
   384	    ``//server/share/...``), or Windows drive-absolute (``C:/...``).
   385	
   386	    These name a location outside the project tree, not a project-relative
   387	    citation. Rule B has no project-declared base to resolve such a path
   388	    against, so — exactly like a `scheme://` URL — it is exempt rather than
   389	    checked (and rather than mis-resolved: `Path(base) / "/abs/x"` silently
   390	    discards `base` and re-checks the literal absolute path against the real
   391	    filesystem, which is not what this rule is verifying). See the module
   392	    docstring for the known-uncovered case this leaves (e.g. an external
   393	    `~/.llm-wiki/...` design-doc store) and for `citations_exempt_external`,
   394	    the coverage field that now counts how many spans this exemption
   395	    swallowed instead of doing so silently.
   396	    """
   397	    return (
   398	        cleaned.startswith("~/")
   399	        or cleaned.startswith("/")
   400	        or bool(WINDOWS_DRIVE_ABS_RE.match(cleaned))
   401	    )
   402	
   403	
   404	def strip_locator_suffix(cleaned: str) -> str:
   405	    """Strip a trailing `::<anchor>`, `:<line>`, or `:<start>-<end>` locator.
   406	
   407	    Reviewers commonly cite a position *within* a file rather than just the
   408	    file itself: `plugins/foo/scripts/check_setup.py:123` (a line number),
   409	    `docs/x.md:10-20` (a line range), or `.hopper/tasks/foo.md::root` (a
   410	    named anchor). The locator is not part of the path — checking the
   411	    literal locator-suffixed string against the filesystem always fails
   412	    even when the file plainly exists. The `::anchor` form is tried first
   413	    since it is the more specific shape (and would otherwise be partially
   414	    consumed by the line-number pattern if the anchor itself looked
   415	    numeric). Returns `cleaned` unchanged if neither suffix is present.
   416	    """
   417	    match = ANCHOR_SUFFIX_RE.search(cleaned)
   418	    if match:
   419	        return cleaned[: match.start()]
   420	    match = LINE_SUFFIX_RE.search(cleaned)
   421	    if match:
   422	        return cleaned[: match.start()]
   423	    return cleaned
   424	
   425	
   426	def pathish_citations(markdown_text: str) -> tuple[list[str], int]:
   427	    """Extract citation spans that look like file paths.
   428	
   429	    Beyond the protocol prefixes, any slash-containing span with a file
   430	    extension, a trailing slash, or a `..` segment is treated as a path so
   431	    that citations of source/test files (e.g. `src/app.py`) are verified too.
   432	    Spans with spaces, URLs, flags, and variables are ignored, as are
   433	    regex/glob patterns (`_looks_like_pattern`), bare-domain URLs
   434	    (`_looks_like_bare_domain`), templated paths containing an
   435	    angle-bracket placeholder (`_looks_like_placeholder`, e.g.
   436	    `goals/<id>/data-contract.md`), and home-relative, POSIX-absolute, or
   437	    Windows-absolute paths naming a location outside the project
   438	    (`_looks_like_out_of_project`, e.g. `~/.llm-wiki/x.md`, `/etc/hosts`, or
   439	    `C:/Users/x`). A line carrying (or immediately following a line that
   440	    carries) the `<!-- verify:ignore -->` marker has all of its citations
   441	    skipped — see module docstring.
   442	
   443	    Returns `(cited, exempt_external)`: `cited` is the list of spans to
   444	    existence-check, and `exempt_external` is a count of spans skipped
   445	    specifically by `_looks_like_out_of_project` (not any other exemption)
   446	    — the caller accumulates this into the `citations_exempt_external`
   447	    coverage field so that exemption is visible in coverage rather than
   448	    disappearing without a trace.
   449	    """
   450	    cited: list[str] = []
   451	    exempt_external = 0
   452	    lines = markdown_text.splitlines()
   453	    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   454	        if IGNORE_MARKER in line or (i > 0 and IGNORE_MARKER in lines[i - 1]):
   455	            continue
   456	        for span in CODE_SPAN.findall(line):
   457	            cleaned = span.strip().replace("\\", "/")
   458	            if not cleaned or " " in cleaned or "://" in cleaned:
   459	                continue
   460	            if cleaned.startswith(("-", "$", "<")):
   461	                continue
   462	            if _looks_like_pattern(cleaned):
   463	                continue
   464	            if _looks_like_bare_domain(cleaned):
   465	                continue
   466	            if _looks_like_placeholder(cleaned):
   467	                continue
   468	            if _looks_like_out_of_project(cleaned):
   469	                exempt_external += 1
   470	                continue
   471	            if cleaned.startswith(PATHISH_PREFIXES):
   472	                cited.append(cleaned)
   473	                continue
   474	            if "/" in cleaned:
   475	                tail = cleaned.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
   476	                if Path(tail).suffix or cleaned.endswith("/") or ".." in cleaned:
   477	                    cited.append(cleaned)
   478	    return cited, exempt_external
   479	
   480	
   481	def submodule_roots(project: Path) -> list[Path]:
   482	    """Every git submodule directory declared in .gitmodules, at any depth.
   483	
   484	    Used as extra resolution bases for Rule B citation existence (not for
   485	    Rule A scope-lock containment). A review may cite a path relative to a
   486	    submodule's own root (e.g. `plugins/harnessloop/` when `harnessloop` is
   487	    checked out as a submodule at <project>/harnessloop) rather than
   488	    relative to the outer project root; without this, such citations are
   489	    dangling relative to every existing base even though the file exists.
   490	
   491	    `.gitmodules` `path =` values are honored verbatim, including nested
   492	    ones (e.g. `path = kernels/openclaw`) — a submodule need not be
   493	    checked out directly under the project root. Projects without a
   494	    .gitmodules file get an empty list and behavior is unchanged.
   495	
   496	    A `path =` value is *canonical-containment-checked* before being
   497	    accepted: if it does not resolve inside `project` under symlink-following
   498	    comparison (`_is_contained`), it is dropped rather than followed.
   499	    `.gitmodules` is ordinary tracked text a review (or an adversarial one)
   500	    could contain a `path = ../outside` entry, and without this check a
   501	    sibling directory outside the project — reachable on the host filesystem
   502	    but not part of it — would become a citation resolution base, letting an
   503	    otherwise-dangling citation resolve against something outside the
   504	    project (TH-0008 REWORK: `submodule_parent_escape`). A lexical-only
   505	    check is not sufficient here either: `path = link` where
   506	    `<project>/link` is itself a symlink to somewhere outside the project
   507	    passes a lexical `normpath` containment test (the symlink's own path is
   508	    inside the project) while its target is not, and `candidate.is_dir()`
   509	    below follows the symlink and reports it as a valid directory regardless
   510	    (T-063 MUST-FIX 2: `symlink_containment_escape`) — `_is_contained`
   511	    resolves the symlink before comparing, so this is rejected too.
   512	    """
   513	    gitmodules = project / ".gitmodules"
   514	    if not gitmodules.is_file():
   515	        return []
   516	    path_re = re.compile(r"^\s*path\s*=\s*(.+?)\s*$")
   517	    roots: list[Path] = []
   518	    for line in gitmodules.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").splitlines():
   519	        match = path_re.match(line)
   520	        if not match:
   521	            continue
   522	        rel = match.group(1).strip().replace("\\", "/")
   523	        if not rel:
   524	            continue
   525	        candidate = project / rel
   526	        normalized = Path(os.path.normpath(str(candidate)))
   527	        if not _is_contained(normalized, project):
   528	            continue
   529	        if candidate.is_dir():
   530	            roots.append(candidate)
   531	    return roots
   532	
   533	
   534	def _git_tracked_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] | None:
   535	    """Git worktree-file index for `build_suffix_index`, or `None` if
   536	    `project` is not itself a git working-tree root (or `git` is
   537	    unavailable).
   538	
   539	    Only engages when `project` *is* the git top level — not merely inside
   540	    one — so a scratch directory nested under some ancestor repository
   541	    (e.g. a test fixture created under this repo's own working tree without
   542	    being `git add`ed) correctly falls back to the walk-based index instead
   543	    of silently seeing zero tracked files and treating every suffix lookup
   544	    as absent.
   545	
   546	    The uniqueness universe is every file that genuinely exists in the
   547	    worktree and is not gitignored — tracked *and* untracked-but-not-ignored
   548	    — not merely `git ls-files`'s tracked set. `git ls-files -z --cached
   549	    --recurse-submodules` supplies the tracked half (including nested
   550	    submodules, any depth); `git ls-files -z --others --exclude-standard`
   551	    supplies untracked-but-not-ignored files in the top-level project (`git`
   552	    has no `--recurse-submodules` support for `--others` — see `git
   553	    help ls-files` — so nested submodules' own untracked files are collected
   554	    separately via `git submodule foreach --recursive`, prefixed with each
   555	    submodule's path). Restricting to tracked files alone (TH-0008 REWORK
   556	    original fix) traded one false-negative for another: a round's just-produced
   557	    evidence/review files are untracked by construction (nothing has `git
   558	    add`ed them yet), so a genuine same-suffix collision between a tracked
   559	    file and such a fresh untracked one was invisible to the ambiguity check
   560	    and a citation that should have been flagged as multiply-resolvable
   561	    instead looked uniquely resolved (T-063 MUST-FIX 1:
   562	    `untracked_pseudo_unique`). Gitignored output (`node_modules/`, `dist/`,
   563	    `build/` artifacts, `.venv/`, ...) is still excluded by `--exclude-standard`,
   564	    so this keeps the benefit of not needing a hardcoded noise-directory
   565	    denylist (see module docstring: "unique" now means unique among every
   566	    real, non-ignored file in the worktree, not literally "git-tracked" nor
   567	    the whole tree including ignored output).
   568	    """
   569	    try:
   570	        toplevel = subprocess.run(
   571	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
   572	            capture_output=True,
   573	            text=True,
   574	            timeout=10,
   575	        )
   576	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   577	        return None
   578	    if toplevel.returncode != 0:
   579	        return None
   580	    top = toplevel.stdout.strip()
   581	    if not top:
   582	        return None
   583	    try:
   584	        if Path(top).resolve() != project.resolve():
   585	            return None
   586	    except OSError:
   587	        return None
   588	
   589	    try:
   590	        tracked = subprocess.run(
   591	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "ls-files", "-z", "--cached", "--recurse-submodules"],
   592	            capture_output=True,
   593	            timeout=60,
   594	        )
   595	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   596	        return None
   597	    if tracked.returncode != 0:
   598	        return None
   599	
   600	    raw_entries: list[bytes] = [seg for seg in tracked.stdout.split(b"\0") if seg]
   601	
   602	    try:
   603	        untracked = subprocess.run(
   604	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "ls-files", "-z", "--others", "--exclude-standard"],
   605	            capture_output=True,
   606	            timeout=60,
   607	        )
   608	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   609	        untracked = None
   610	    if untracked is not None and untracked.returncode == 0:
   611	        raw_entries.extend(seg for seg in untracked.stdout.split(b"\0") if seg)
   612	
   613	    # Nested submodules' own untracked-but-not-ignored files: `--others` has no
   614	    # `--recurse-submodules` mode, so shell out to `submodule foreach --recursive`
   615	    # and run the same lookup inside each, prefixing with $displaypath (the
   616	    # submodule's path relative to `project`). A read loop keyed on NUL, not
   617	    # newline, keeps this safe for filenames containing embedded newlines, and
   618	    # `printf ... \0` re-delimits with NUL so the outer split below still works.
   619	    try:
   620	        nested = subprocess.run(
   621	            [
   622	                "git", "-C", str(project), "submodule", "foreach", "--recursive", "-q",
   623	                'git ls-files -z --others --exclude-standard | '
   624	                'while IFS= read -r -d "" f; do printf "%s/%s\\0" "$displaypath" "$f"; done',
   625	            ],
   626	            capture_output=True,
   627	            timeout=60,
   628	        )
   629	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   630	        nested = None
   631	    if nested is not None and nested.returncode == 0:
   632	        raw_entries.extend(seg for seg in nested.stdout.split(b"\0") if seg)
   633	
   634	    index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] = {}
   635	    seen: set[str] = set()
   636	    for raw in raw_entries:
   637	        try:
   638	            rel = raw.decode("utf-8")
   639	        except UnicodeDecodeError:
   640	            continue
   641	        if rel in seen:
   642	            continue
   643	        seen.add(rel)
   644	        parts = tuple(p for p in rel.split("/") if p)
   645	        if not parts:
   646	            continue
   647	        index.setdefault(parts[-1], []).append(parts)
   648	    return index
   649	
   650	
   651	def build_suffix_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]]:
   652	    """Index files under `project` by basename, once per run.
   653	
   654	    Backs the Rule B suffix-unique fallback (`suffix_unique_match`): after a
   655	    citation fails to resolve against every explicit base (project root,
   656	    goal/round dirs, `.harnessloop/`, submodule roots — with and without a
   657	    locator suffix stripped), it is tried once more as a path *suffix*
   658	    against this index. Building it once per `verify_project` call (rather
   659	    than walking the tree per citation) keeps that fallback from turning an
   660	    O(citations) check into an O(citations * tree size) one.
   661	
   662	    Primary source is the tracked-plus-untracked-not-ignored worktree index
   663	    (`_git_tracked_index`, see its docstring), used whenever `project` is
   664	    itself a git working-tree root. Falls back to
   665	    walking the filesystem directly — `os.walk` with in-place `dirnames`
   666	    pruning of `NOISE_DIR_NAMES` (`.git`, `node_modules`, build output,
   667	    venvs, ...), not `Path.rglob("*")`, since a project with vendored
   668	    dependencies can have orders of magnitude more files inside those
   669	    directories than in the rest of the tree — when `project` is not a git
   670	    working-tree root or `git` is unavailable. Each entry maps a basename to
   671	    the list of path-segment tuples (relative to `project`) of every
   672	    indexed file with that basename. Note that an indexed entry is not, by
   673	    itself, proof the file still exists: `suffix_unique_match` re-verifies
   674	    the specific matched path against the real filesystem before accepting
   675	    it (see its docstring).
   676	    """
   677	    tracked = _git_tracked_index(project)
   678	    if tracked is not None:
   679	        return tracked
   680	
   681	    index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] = {}
   682	    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(project):
   683	        dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in NOISE_DIR_NAMES]
   684	        rel_dir = Path(dirpath).relative_to(project)
   685	        dir_parts = () if str(rel_dir) == "." else rel_dir.parts
   686	        for filename in filenames:
   687	            parts = dir_parts + (filename,)
   688	            index.setdefault(filename, []).append(parts)
   689	    return index
   690	
   691	
   692	def _exists_as(path: Path, want_dir: bool) -> bool:
   693	    """Existence check honoring trailing-slash directory semantics.
   694	
   695	    A citation ending in `/` names a directory: it must resolve to an
   696	    actual directory (`is_dir()`), not merely to any filesystem entry of
   697	    the same name. Without this, `pkg/real.md/` would resolve against the
   698	    *file* `pkg/real.md` via plain `exists()` (TH-0008 REWORK:
   699	    `trailing_slash_file`).
   700	    """
   701	    if want_dir:
   702	        return path.is_dir()
   703	    return path.exists()
   704	
   705	
   706	def suffix_unique_match(
   707	    cleaned: str, index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]], project: Path
   708	) -> bool:
   709	    """True if `cleaned` matches exactly one indexed file as a path
   710	    *suffix*, and that specific file still actually exists.
   711	
   712	    Last-resort Rule B fallback (see module docstring) for a citation that
   713	    is correct but written relative to none of the explicit bases — e.g.
   714	    `harnessloop-setup/SKILL.md` for a file that actually lives at
   715	    `plugins/harnessloop/skills/harnessloop-setup/SKILL.md`. Deliberately
   716	    conservative in four ways, each guarding a specific false-negative
   717	    risk:
   718	
   719	    - Comparison is by path *segment*, not raw string suffix — `os.html`
   720	      must not match `.../macos.html` (string `endswith` would).
   721	    - At least two path segments are required — a single bare filename
   722	      (`agents/`-style single component citations, or a bare basename like
   723	      `verify_protocol.py`) is the shape most likely to be a typo and least
   724	      likely to disambiguate anything, so it never qualifies here; it is
   725	      still checked (and can still fail) against the explicit bases above.
   726	    - The match must be *unique* across the index — 0 hits is still
   727	      dangling (no such file), and >=2 hits is also still dangling (an
   728	      ambiguous suffix is not a resolved citation; picking one of several
   729	      candidates would silently paper over which file was meant).
   730	    - The unique match's specific path is re-checked against the real
   731	      filesystem before being accepted — `is_dir()` if `cleaned` ends in
   732	      `/`, otherwise `exists()` (`_exists_as`) — rather than trusted purely
   733	      because the index once recorded it. The index can otherwise be
   734	      stale (a file the index recorded can be deleted between
   735	      `build_suffix_index` and this call — TH-0008 REWORK:
   736	      `stale_index_after_delete`) or, when it was built by walking the
   737	      filesystem, can contain a broken symlink that `os.walk` lists in
   738	      `filenames` without ever following it (TH-0008 REWORK:
   739	      `broken_symlink`); either way, treating the indexed entry as
   740	      sufficient proof of existence would accept a citation to something
   741	      that, right now, does not resolve to a real file.
   742	
   743	    A fifth guard, added in T-063 MUST-FIX 2 (`symlink_containment_escape`):
   744	    the unique match's specific path is also *canonical-containment-checked*
   745	    (`_is_contained`) against `project` before being accepted, not merely
   746	    assumed safe because it came from the index. The index is built from
   747	    `git ls-files`, which lists a tracked symlink like any other tracked
   748	    entry — a citation ending in, say, `pkg/external.md` where
   749	    `<project>/deep/pkg/external.md` is itself a symlink pointing at a real
   750	    file *outside* the project would otherwise pass the existence check
   751	    (the symlink's target genuinely exists) while resolving to something
   752	    the project does not control. Containment is checked first and
   753	    existence second, deliberately not combined into one step: a broken
   754	    symlink still passes containment (its lexical path is inside the
   755	    project even though nothing exists at the far end) and is correctly
   756	    caught by the existence check that follows, exactly as before.
   757	    """
   758	    parts = tuple(p for p in cleaned.split("/") if p)
   759	    if len(parts) < 2:
   760	        return False
   760	        return False
   761	    candidates = index.get(parts[-1], [])
   762	    matches = [c for c in candidates if len(c) >= len(parts) and c[-len(parts) :] == parts]
   763	    if len(matches) != 1:
   764	        return False
   765	    match_path = project.joinpath(*matches[0])
   766	    if not _is_contained(match_path, project):
   767	        return False
   768	    return _exists_as(match_path, cleaned.endswith("/"))
   769	
   770	
   771	def _resolve_in_project(base: Path, cited: str, project: Path) -> Path | None:
   772	    """Join `base` and `cited`, normalize, and return the result only if it
   773	    stays within `project` under *canonical* containment — otherwise `None`.
   774	
   775	    Guards against a citation containing `../` segments walking the join
   776	    outside the project tree, where `Path.exists()` would silently consult
   777	    the real host filesystem for an unrelated path that happens to share a
   778	    name with something the review meant to cite (TH-0008 REWORK:
   779	    `submodule_parent_escape` — the same containment discipline
   780	    `submodule_roots` applies to `.gitmodules` entries, applied here to
   781	    every citation resolution). Lexical `normpath` containment alone is not
   782	    enough for a project-internal symlink whose target lives outside the
   783	    project (e.g. `<project>/link -> /outside`, cited as `link/pkg/x.py`):
   784	    the joined-and-normalized candidate's *path* is lexically inside the
   785	    project even though what it resolves to is not (T-063 MUST-FIX 2:
   786	    `symlink_containment_escape`) — `_is_contained` resolves both sides
   787	    before comparing, so this is rejected too. The returned `candidate` is
   788	    still the lexical (non-canonical) join, not the resolved path: callers
   789	    only need it for the subsequent `_exists_as` check, which itself
   790	    follows symlinks via `Path.exists()` / `is_dir()`.
   791	    """
   792	    candidate = Path(os.path.normpath(str(base / cited)))
   793	    if not _is_contained(candidate, project):
   794	        return None
   795	    return candidate
   796	
   797	
   798	def _any_base_resolves(cited: str, bases: list[Path], project: Path, want_dir: bool) -> bool:
   799	    """True if `cited` resolves (containment-checked, directory-semantics-
   800	    aware) against any of `bases`."""
   801	    for base in bases:
   802	        resolved = _resolve_in_project(base, cited, project)
   803	        if resolved is not None and _exists_as(resolved, want_dir):
   804	            return True
   805	    return False
   806	
   807	
   808	def verify_round(
   809	    project: Path, round_dir: Path, suffix_index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]]
   810	) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
   811	    violations: list[dict] = []
   812	    goal_dir = round_dir.parent.parent
   813	    bases = [project, goal_dir, round_dir]
   814	    # Rule B only: extra resolution bases for (1) citations that use one of
   815	    # the PATHISH_PREFIXES verbatim (setup/, state/, goals/, ...) — those
   816	    # directories actually live under <project>/.harnessloop/, which none of
   817	    # `bases` covers on their own — and (2) citations written relative to a
   818	    # submodule's own root. Kept separate from `bases` so Rule A's
   819	    # scope-lock containment check is not loosened by either addition.
   820	    citation_bases = bases + [project / ".harnessloop"] + submodule_roots(project)
   821	
   822	    checked_files = [
   823	        path
   824	        for sub in ("evidence", "reviews")
   825	        for path in sorted((round_dir / sub).rglob("*"))
   826	        if path.is_file()
   827	    ]
   828	
   829	    coverage = _empty_coverage()
   830	    coverage["rounds"] = 1
   831	    if not checked_files:
   832	        coverage["rounds_zero_inspected"] = 1
   833	
   834	    # E2(a): scope-lock existence and Allowed-Changes parseability are
   835	    # checked unconditionally for every round, independent of whether the
   836	    # round has any evidence/review files. Before this change both checks
   837	    # lived behind `if checked_files:`, so a round with nothing under
   838	    # evidence/ or reviews/ never had its scope-lock inspected at all (the
   839	    # historical "9 rounds zero-inspected, still exit 0" gap). Containment
   840	    # (below) still requires artifacts to check, so it stays guarded.
   841	    scope_lock = round_dir / "scope-lock.md"
   842	    spans: list[str] = []
   843	    if not scope_lock.exists():
   844	        violations.append(
   845	            {
   846	                "round": str(round_dir),
   847	                "kind": "missing-scope-lock",
   848	                "detail": f"{scope_lock} does not exist",
   849	            }
   850	        )
   851	    else:
   852	        spans = extract_allowed_spans(scope_lock.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   853	        if not spans:
   854	            violations.append(
   855	                {
   856	                    "round": str(round_dir),
   857	                    "kind": "unparseable-allowed-changes",
   858	                    "detail": f"no backtick path spans found under '## Allowed Changes' in {scope_lock}",
   859	                }
   860	            )
   861	
   862	    if checked_files and spans:
   863	        coverage["rule_a_files"] = len(checked_files)
   864	        for file_path in checked_files:
   865	            allowed = any(
   866	                is_under(file_path, base / span)
   867	                for base in bases
   868	                for span in spans
   869	            )
   870	            if not allowed:
   871	                violations.append(
   872	                    {
   873	                        "round": str(round_dir),
   874	                        "kind": "scope-lock-violation",
   875	                        "detail": f"{file_path} is outside every allowed path in {scope_lock}",
   876	                    }
   877	                )
   878	
   879	    reviews_dir = round_dir / "reviews"
   880	    if reviews_dir.is_dir():
   881	        for review in sorted(reviews_dir.rglob("*.md")):
   882	            coverage["rule_b_files"] += 1
   883	            cited_list, exempt_external = pathish_citations(review.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   884	            coverage["citations_exempt_external"] += exempt_external
   885	            for cited in cited_list:
   886	                coverage["citations_checked"] += 1
   887	                want_dir = cited.endswith("/")
   888	                resolved = _any_base_resolves(cited, citation_bases, project, want_dir)
   889	                stripped = cited
   890	                if not resolved:
   891	                    stripped = strip_locator_suffix(cited)
   892	                    if stripped != cited:
   893	                        resolved = _any_base_resolves(stripped, citation_bases, project, want_dir)
   894	                if not resolved:
   895	                    resolved = suffix_unique_match(stripped, suffix_index, project)
   896	                if not resolved:
   897	                    violations.append(
   898	                        {
   899	                            "round": str(round_dir),
   900	                            "kind": "dangling-citation",
   901	                            "detail": f"{review} cites `{cited}` which does not exist",
   902	                        }
   903	                    )
   904	
   905	    # E4: same-file enum contradiction in decision.md. Deliberately the
   906	    # narrowest possible check — two enum lines in one file, compared after
   907	    # strip().lower(). No prose parsing, no path resolution, no cross-file
   908	    # join, no value normalization: those are exactly what produced six of
   909	    # this repo's ten evolution issues. Absent fields are never a violation,
   910	    # so existing rounds need no migration.
   911	    decision = round_dir / "decision.md"
   912	    if decision.exists():
   913	        verdict = residuals = None
   914	        for line in decision.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore").splitlines():
   915	            stripped = line.strip()
   916	            if verdict is None and stripped.lower().startswith("- verdict:"):
   917	                verdict = stripped.split(":", 1)[1].strip().lower()
   918	            elif residuals is None and stripped.lower().startswith("- residuals:"):
   919	                residuals = stripped.split(":", 1)[1].strip().lower()
   920	        if verdict == "pass" and residuals not in (None, "", "none"):
   921	            violations.append(
   922	                {
   923	                    "round": str(round_dir),
   924	                    "kind": "verdict-residual-contradiction",
   925	                    "detail": (
   926	                        f"{decision} declares `Verdict: pass` while `Residuals` is "
   927	                        f"non-none — use `pass-with-residual` when part of the claim "
   928	                        f"is uncovered or deferred"
   929	                    ),
   930	                }
   931	            )
   932	
   933	    return violations, coverage
   934	
   935	
   936	def _empty_coverage() -> dict:
   937	    """Zeroed coverage accumulator. Keys match the `### Mechanical Gate
   938	    Boundary` IN column in harnessloop-loop/SKILL.md one-to-one; a rename on
   939	    either side must update the other."""
   940	    return {
   941	        "rounds": 0,
   942	        "rounds_zero_inspected": 0,
   943	        "rule_a_files": 0,
   944	        "rule_b_files": 0,
   945	        "citations_checked": 0,
   946	        "citations_exempt_external": 0,
   947	    }
   948	
   949	
   950	def verify_project(project: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
   951	    goals_dir = project / ".harnessloop" / "goals"
   952	    coverage = _empty_coverage()
   953	    if not goals_dir.is_dir():
   954	        return [], coverage
   955	    violations: list[dict] = []
   956	    # Built once per project run (not per round/citation) — see
   957	    # `build_suffix_index` for why this matters for performance.
   958	    suffix_index = build_suffix_index(project)
   959	    for round_dir in sorted(goals_dir.glob("*/rounds/*")):
   960	        if round_dir.is_dir():
   961	            round_violations, round_coverage = verify_round(project, round_dir, suffix_index)
   962	            violations.extend(round_violations)
   963	            for key in coverage:
   964	                coverage[key] += round_coverage[key]
   965	    return violations, coverage
   966

   980	            "citations using a PATHISH_PREFIXES prefix verbatim, e.g. "

   214	# Explicit per-line opt-out for Rule B: a review line that carries this
   215	# marker (or is immediately preceded by a line that does) has every
   216	# citation on it skipped. See module docstring for when to use it.
   217	IGNORE_MARKER = "<!-- verify:ignore -->"
   218	
   219	# Regex/glob metacharacters. A backtick span containing any of these is a
   220	# quoted pattern (regex alternation, anchors, glob wildcards, ...), not a
   221	# literal path, so it is never treated as a citation.
   222	PATH_META_CHARS = frozenset("^$*?|(){}[]\\+")
   223	
   224	# A bare (schemeless) domain: at least two dot-separated alnum/hyphen
   225	# labels, e.g. "docs.python.org" or "github.com". Anchored so it must
   226	# consume the whole first path segment — this deliberately does not match
   227	# a plain filename with an extension (e.g. "package.json" has no `/`
   228	# after it, and is never even offered to this check; see
   229	# _looks_like_bare_domain).
   230	BARE_DOMAIN_RE = re.compile(
   231	    r"^[A-Za-z0-9](?:[A-Za-z0-9-]*[A-Za-z0-9])?"
   232	    r"(?:\.[A-Za-z0-9](?:[A-Za-z0-9-]*[A-Za-z0-9])?)+$"
   233	)
   234	
   235	# A Windows drive-letter absolute path, e.g. `C:/Users/x` -- checked after
   236	# `pathish_citations` has already normalized backslashes to `/`, so the
   237	# original `C:\Users\x` form is matched by this too. See
   238	# `_looks_like_out_of_project`.
   239	WINDOWS_DRIVE_ABS_RE = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z]:/")
   240	
   241	# Trailing locator suffixes stripped before existence checking: `::anchor`
   242	# (checked first, since it is the more specific shape) and `:line` /
   243	# `:start-end`. See `strip_locator_suffix`.
   244	ANCHOR_SUFFIX_RE = re.compile(r"::[^:]+$")
   245	LINE_SUFFIX_RE = re.compile(r":\d+(?:-\d+)?$")
   246	
   247	# Noise directories excluded from the Rule B suffix-unique fallback index
   248	# (`build_suffix_index`): build output, vendored/installed dependencies, and
   249	# VCS internals are never what a review is citing, and including them would
   250	# both slow the index and manufacture spurious "unique" matches inside
   251	# generated trees that happen to mirror source filenames.
   252	NOISE_DIR_NAMES = frozenset(
   253	    {
   254	        ".git",
   255	        "node_modules",
   256	        "dist",
   257	        "build",
   258	        "bin",
   259	        "obj",
   260	        ".venv",
   261	        "__pycache__",
   262	        ".artifacts",
   263	        "coverage",
   264	    }
   265	)

   267	
   268	def norm(path: Path) -> str:
   269	    return os.path.normcase(os.path.normpath(str(path)))
   270	
   271	
   272	def is_under(child: Path, parent: Path) -> bool:
   273	    child_s, parent_s = norm(child), norm(parent)
   274	    return child_s == parent_s or child_s.startswith(parent_s + os.sep)
   275	
   276	
   277	def _canonical(path: Path) -> Path:
   278	    """Resolve `path` to its canonical (symlink-following) form.
   279	
   280	    `strict=False` normalizes past a broken symlink or a nonexistent
   281	    component instead of raising — existence is a separate concern, owned
   282	    by `_exists_as` at the point a match is actually accepted, not by this
   283	    resolution step (T-063 MUST-FIX 2: canonical containment and match-time
   284	    existence re-verification must not be conflated with each other).
   285	    """
   286	    return path.resolve(strict=False)
   287	
   288	
   289	def _is_contained(candidate: Path, project: Path) -> bool:
   290	    """True if `candidate` lands inside `project` under *canonical*
   291	    (symlink-resolved) comparison of both sides.
   292	
   293	    Lexical `normpath` containment (`is_under` alone) is not enough: a
   294	    project-internal symlink whose path is inside the project but whose
   295	    target is not (e.g. `<project>/link -> /outside`, or a `.gitmodules`
   296	    `path =` entry naming such a symlink) passes a lexical check while
   297	    actually resolving outside the project entirely (T-063 MUST-FIX 2:
   298	    `symlink_containment_escape`). `project` itself is resolved too, not
   299	    just `candidate` — on macOS `/tmp` is a symlink to `/private/tmp`, so a
   300	    project rooted there would otherwise be compared against an unresolved
   301	    parent and see a spurious mismatch (or, worse, a spurious escape) for
   302	    every candidate. Used by every containment check that guards Rule B
   303	    citation resolution: `_resolve_in_project` (explicit-base and suffix
   304	    resolution), `submodule_roots` (`.gitmodules` `path =` acceptance), and
   305	    `suffix_unique_match` (the unique-hit's specific path) — all three must
   306	    share this one definition so a symlink escape cannot slip through
   307	    whichever path is not updated.
   308	    """
   309	    return is_under(_canonical(candidate), _canonical(project))
   310	
   311	
   312	def extract_allowed_spans(scope_lock_text: str) -> list[str]:
   313	    """Collect allowed paths from the '## Allowed Changes' section.
   314	
   315	    Accepts both formats in the wild: backtick path spans in prose/bullets,
   316	    and the scope-lock-template.md table whose first column is the path.
   317	    """
   318	    spans: list[str] = []
   319	    in_section = False
   320	    for line in scope_lock_text.splitlines():
   321	        stripped = line.strip()
   322	        if stripped.startswith("## "):
   323	            in_section = stripped.lower() == "## allowed changes"
   324	            continue
   325	        if not in_section:
   326	            continue
   327	        spans.extend(CODE_SPAN.findall(line))
   328	        if stripped.startswith("|"):
   329	            cells = [c.strip() for c in stripped.strip("|").split("|")]
   330	            if not cells:
   480	
   481	def submodule_roots(project: Path) -> list[Path]:
   482	    """Every git submodule directory declared in .gitmodules, at any depth.
   483	
   484	    Used as extra resolution bases for Rule B citation existence (not for
   485	    Rule A scope-lock containment). A review may cite a path relative to a
   486	    submodule's own root (e.g. `plugins/harnessloop/` when `harnessloop` is
   487	    checked out as a submodule at <project>/harnessloop) rather than
   488	    relative to the outer project root; without this, such citations are
   489	    dangling relative to every existing base even though the file exists.
   490	
   491	    `.gitmodules` `path =` values are honored verbatim, including nested
   492	    ones (e.g. `path = kernels/openclaw`) — a submodule need not be
   493	    checked out directly under the project root. Projects without a
   494	    .gitmodules file get an empty list and behavior is unchanged.
   495	
   496	    A `path =` value is *canonical-containment-checked* before being
   497	    accepted: if it does not resolve inside `project` under symlink-following
   498	    comparison (`_is_contained`), it is dropped rather than followed.
   499	    `.gitmodules` is ordinary tracked text a review (or an adversarial one)
   500	    could contain a `path = ../outside` entry, and without this check a
   501	    sibling directory outside the project — reachable on the host filesystem
   502	    but not part of it — would become a citation resolution base, letting an
   503	    otherwise-dangling citation resolve against something outside the
   504	    project (TH-0008 REWORK: `submodule_parent_escape`). A lexical-only
   505	    check is not sufficient here either: `path = link` where
   506	    `<project>/link` is itself a symlink to somewhere outside the project
   507	    passes a lexical `normpath` containment test (the symlink's own path is
   508	    inside the project) while its target is not, and `candidate.is_dir()`
   509	    below follows the symlink and reports it as a valid directory regardless
   510	    (T-063 MUST-FIX 2: `symlink_containment_escape`) — `_is_contained`
   511	    resolves the symlink before comparing, so this is rejected too.
   512	    """
   513	    gitmodules = project / ".gitmodules"
   514	    if not gitmodules.is_file():
   515	        return []
   516	    path_re = re.compile(r"^\s*path\s*=\s*(.+?)\s*$")
   517	    roots: list[Path] = []
   518	    for line in gitmodules.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").splitlines():
   519	        match = path_re.match(line)
   520	        if not match:
   521	            continue
   522	        rel = match.group(1).strip().replace("\\", "/")
   523	        if not rel:
   524	            continue
   525	        candidate = project / rel
   526	        normalized = Path(os.path.normpath(str(candidate)))
   527	        if not _is_contained(normalized, project):
   528	            continue
   529	        if candidate.is_dir():
   530	            roots.append(candidate)
   531	    return roots
   532	
   533	
   534	def _git_tracked_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] | None:
   535	    """Git worktree-file index for `build_suffix_index`, or `None` if
   536	    `project` is not itself a git working-tree root (or `git` is
   537	    unavailable).
   538	
   539	    Only engages when `project` *is* the git top level — not merely inside
   540	    one — so a scratch directory nested under some ancestor repository
   541	    (e.g. a test fixture created under this repo's own working tree without
   542	    being `git add`ed) correctly falls back to the walk-based index instead
   543	    of silently seeing zero tracked files and treating every suffix lookup
   544	    as absent.
   545	
   546	    The uniqueness universe is every file that genuinely exists in the
   547	    worktree and is not gitignored — tracked *and* untracked-but-not-ignored
   548	    — not merely `git ls-files`'s tracked set. `git ls-files -z --cached
   549	    --recurse-submodules` supplies the tracked half (including nested
   550	    submodules, any depth); `git ls-files -z --others --exclude-standard`
   551	    supplies untracked-but-not-ignored files in the top-level project (`git`
   552	    has no `--recurse-submodules` support for `--others` — see `git
   553	    help ls-files` — so nested submodules' own untracked files are collected
   554	    separately via `git submodule foreach --recursive`, prefixed with each
   555	    submodule's path). Restricting to tracked files alone (TH-0008 REWORK
   556	    original fix) traded one false-negative for another: a round's just-produced
   557	    evidence/review files are untracked by construction (nothing has `git
   558	    add`ed them yet), so a genuine same-suffix collision between a tracked
   559	    file and such a fresh untracked one was invisible to the ambiguity check
   560	    and a citation that should have been flagged as multiply-resolvable
   561	    instead looked uniquely resolved (T-063 MUST-FIX 1:
   562	    `untracked_pseudo_unique`). Gitignored output (`node_modules/`, `dist/`,
   563	    `build/` artifacts, `.venv/`, ...) is still excluded by `--exclude-standard`,
   564	    so this keeps the benefit of not needing a hardcoded noise-directory
   565	    denylist (see module docstring: "unique" now means unique among every
   566	    real, non-ignored file in the worktree, not literally "git-tracked" nor
   567	    the whole tree including ignored output).
   568	    """
   569	    try:
   570	        toplevel = subprocess.run(
   571	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
   572	            capture_output=True,
   573	            text=True,
   574	            timeout=10,
   575	        )
   576	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   577	        return None
   578	    if toplevel.returncode != 0:
   579	        return None
   580	    top = toplevel.stdout.strip()
   581	    if not top:
   582	        return None
   583	    try:
   584	        if Path(top).resolve() != project.resolve():
   585	            return None
   586	    except OSError:
   587	        return None
   588	
   589	    try:
   590	        tracked = subprocess.run(
   591	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "ls-files", "-z", "--cached", "--recurse-submodules"],
   592	            capture_output=True,
   593	            timeout=60,
   594	        )
   595	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   596	        return None
   597	    if tracked.returncode != 0:
   598	        return None
   599	
   600	    raw_entries: list[bytes] = [seg for seg in tracked.stdout.split(b"\0") if seg]
   601	
   602	    try:
   603	        untracked = subprocess.run(
   604	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "ls-files", "-z", "--others", "--exclude-standard"],
   605	            capture_output=True,
   606	            timeout=60,
   607	        )
   608	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   609	        untracked = None
   610	    if untracked is not None and untracked.returncode == 0:
   611	        raw_entries.extend(seg for seg in untracked.stdout.split(b"\0") if seg)
   612	
   613	    # Nested submodules' own untracked-but-not-ignored files: `--others` has no
   614	    # `--recurse-submodules` mode, so shell out to `submodule foreach --recursive`
   615	    # and run the same lookup inside each, prefixing with $displaypath (the
   616	    # submodule's path relative to `project`). A read loop keyed on NUL, not
   617	    # newline, keeps this safe for filenames containing embedded newlines, and
   618	    # `printf ... \0` re-delimits with NUL so the outer split below still works.
   619	    try:
   620	        nested = subprocess.run(
   621	            [
   622	                "git", "-C", str(project), "submodule", "foreach", "--recursive", "-q",
   623	                'git ls-files -z --others --exclude-standard | '
   624	                'while IFS= read -r -d "" f; do printf "%s/%s\\0" "$displaypath" "$f"; done',
   625	            ],
   626	            capture_output=True,
   627	            timeout=60,
   628	        )
   629	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   630	        nested = None
   631	    if nested is not None and nested.returncode == 0:
   632	        raw_entries.extend(seg for seg in nested.stdout.split(b"\0") if seg)
   633	
   634	    index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] = {}
   635	    seen: set[str] = set()
   636	    for raw in raw_entries:
   637	        try:
   638	            rel = raw.decode("utf-8")
   639	        except UnicodeDecodeError:
   640	            continue
   641	        if rel in seen:
   642	            continue
   643	        seen.add(rel)
   644	        parts = tuple(p for p in rel.split("/") if p)
   645	        if not parts:
   646	            continue
   647	        index.setdefault(parts[-1], []).append(parts)
   648	    return index
   649	
   650	
   651	def build_suffix_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]]:
   652	    """Index files under `project` by basename, once per run.
   653	
   654	    Backs the Rule B suffix-unique fallback (`suffix_unique_match`): after a
   655	    citation fails to resolve against every explicit base (project root,
   968	def main() -> int:
   969	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
   970	        description=(
   971	            "Verify mechanical Harnessloop protocol gates (scope-lock containment "
   972	            "and citation existence). Citation spans that are regex/glob patterns, "
   973	            "bare-domain URLs (docs.python.org/..., github.com/...), or templated "
   974	            "paths with an angle-bracket placeholder (goals/<id>/...) are exempt "
   975	            "automatically; a citation known to be intentional prose rather than a "
   976	            "real reference can be exempted explicitly by putting "
   977	            "'<!-- verify:ignore -->' on the same line or the line before it. "
   978	            "Citations are resolved against the project root, the round's goal and "
   979	            "round directories, the project's own .harnessloop/ directory (for "
   980	            "citations using a PATHISH_PREFIXES prefix verbatim, e.g. "
   981	            "setup/data-sources.md), and the root of every git submodule (any depth) "
   982	            "declared in the project's .gitmodules (canonical-containment-checked: a path "
   983	            "or .gitmodules entry that would resolve outside the project — including via "
   984	            "a project-internal symlink — is never treated as resolved). A trailing "
   985	            ":<line>, :<start>-<end>, or ::<anchor> locator is stripped before checking. "
   986	            "If still unresolved, a citation with >=2 path "
   987	            "segments that matches exactly one file in the project's tracked-plus-"
   988	            "untracked-not-ignored worktree index "
   989	            "(or, outside a git working tree, the walked and noise-pruned tree) as a "
   990	            "path suffix, and whose matched path still actually exists, is also "
   991	            "accepted. A citation ending in / must resolve to a directory. Home-relative "
   992	            "(~/...), filesystem-absolute (/...), and Windows-absolute (C:/..., "
   993	            "\\\\server\\share...) citations are exempt (out of project scope; counted "
   994	            "in the citations_exempt_external coverage field)."
   995	        )
   996	    )
   997	    parser.add_argument("--project", "-p", default=".", help="Target project directory. Defaults to current directory.")
   998	    parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Print machine-readable JSON output.")
   999	    args = parser.parse_args()
  1000	
  1001	    project = Path(args.project).resolve()
  1002	    if not project.is_dir():
  1003	        print(f"Project directory not found: {project}", file=sys.stderr)
  1004	        return 2
  1005	
  1006	    violations, coverage = verify_project(project)
  1007	    if args.json:
  1008	        print(
  1009	            json.dumps(
  1010	                {"project": str(project), "violations": violations, "coverage": coverage},
  1011	                indent=2,
  1012	                ensure_ascii=False,
  1013	            )
  1014	        )
  1015	    else:
  1016	        print(f"Harnessloop verify: {project}")
  1017	        if violations:
  1018	            for violation in violations:
  1019	                print(f"  [{violation['kind']}] {violation['detail']}")
  1020	            print(f"{len(violations)} violation(s) found.")
  1021	        elif coverage["rounds_zero_inspected"] > 0:
  1022	            # E2(b): a clean exit is not a blanket "all clear" when some
  1023	            # rounds had nothing under evidence/ or reviews/ to check in the
  1024	            # first place. Print the qualified form instead of the
  1025	            # unqualified banner below so a clean exit for those rounds
  1026	            # cannot be misread as "checked and clean".
  1027	            print(
  1028	                "passed, but not a clean sweep: "
  1029	                f"{coverage['rounds_zero_inspected']} of {coverage['rounds']} round(s) had "
  1030	                "nothing under evidence/ or reviews/ to inspect — a clean exit for those "
  1031	                'rounds means "nothing to check", not "checked and clean".'
  1032	            )
  1033	        else:
  1034	            print("All mechanical protocol gates passed.")
  1035	        # Unconditional coverage line (E2(b)): printed regardless of pass/fail
  1036	        # so "was this gate actually run over anything" is always visible,
  1037	        # not just inferable from exit code. Field names here are the short
  1038	        # print-form of the `coverage` dict keys (see `_empty_coverage`);
  1039	        # the --json output uses the full dict verbatim.
  1040	        print(
  1041	            "coverage: "
  1042	            f"rounds={coverage['rounds']} rule_a_files={coverage['rule_a_files']} "
  1043	            f"rule_b_files={coverage['rule_b_files']} citations={coverage['citations_checked']} "
  1044	            f"citations_exempt_external={coverage['citations_exempt_external']} "
  1045	            f"zero_inspected={coverage['rounds_zero_inspected']}"
  1046	        )
  1047	    return 1 if violations else 0
  1048	
  1049	
  1050	if __name__ == "__main__":
  1051	    raise SystemExit(main())
